plain.file.GettingStarted.html	1678 chars, CRLF 0, ends with newline 1
  |<!DOCTYPE html>
  |<html>
  |    <head>
  |        <meta charset="utf-8">
  |        <title>Getting Started with the k17xl Excel Add-In</title>
  |    </head>
  |    <body>
  |        <h1>Getting Started with the k17xl Excel Add-In</h1>
  |        <p>JGraph's compiler creates Excel add-ins that can be integrated with Excel applications. You can use this guide to set up your environment.</p>
  |        <h2>Location of Installed Excel Component Files</h2>
  |        <p>View k17xl Files</p>
  |        <h2>Prerequisites</h2>
  |        <p>Install the JGraph Runtime 0.2.3 on the computer that runs Excel, or set JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to the folder of a runtime carried along.</p>
  |        <p>Complete this step only if you have not installed the JGraph Runtime while installing the add-in.</p>
  |        <h2>Register the Add-In</h2>
  |        <p>Run _install.bat beside the add-in's DLL once: as an administrator it registers the component for every user, otherwise for the current user alone. Excel installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for Excel run it as an administrator.</p>
  |        <h2>Use the Add-In in Excel</h2>
  |        <p>Open k17xl.xlam in Excel, or add it under File &gt; Options &gt; Add-ins; its worksheet functions, one per exported function, can then be used in any workbook's cells. The module itself is k17xl.bas, which can be imported into a workbook's own VBA project instead.</p>
  |        <h2>Additional Resources</h2>
  |        <p>The deployment chapter of the JGraph scripting guide (docs/jgs-scripting-guide.html) says how the add-in's functions are called from worksheet cells and from VBA, and how the add-in is distributed.</p>
  |    </body>
  |</html>
plain.file._install.bat	880 chars, CRLF 0, ends with newline 0
  |@ECHO OFF
  |
  |:START
  |SETLOCAL
  |set FILE=k17xl_1_0.dll
  |ECHO Registering %FILE%...
  |where MWRegsvr 1>NUL 2>&1
  |if ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO USEREGSVR
  |
  |MWRegsvr %FILE%
  |IF ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO :USERONLYREG
  |GOTO :SUCCESS
  |
  |:USERONLYREG
  |ECHO.
  |SET YOURINPUT=
  |Set /P YOURINPUT=Do you want to register the component for YOU only? [Y]es or [N]o:
  |IF NOT "%YOURINPUT%"=="" SET YOURINPUT=%YOURINPUT:~0,1%
  |IF /I "%YOURINPUT%" == "Y" (
  |  ECHO Registering the component for you only...
  |    MWRegsvr /useronly %FILE%
  |  if ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO FAILURE
  |  GOTO SUCCESS
  |)
  |ECHO Did not register the component.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:USEREGSVR
  |REM if mwregsvr is not available use regsvr32
  |ECHO Using regsvr32 to register the component...
  |regsvr32 %FILE%
  |GOTO SUCCESS
  |
  |:FAILURE
  |ECHO Failed to register the component.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:SUCCESS
  |ECHO Please refer to the documentation for any additional setup steps.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:END
  |ENDLOCAL
  |ECHO.

plain.file.readme.txt	1434 chars, CRLF 30, ends with newline 1
  |k17xl JGraph Excel Add-in 
  |
  |1. Prerequisites for Deployment 
  |
  |Verify that the JGraph Runtime (0.2.3) is installed on the computer that
  |runs Excel. If not, install it from its installer, or set JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to the folder
  |of a JGraph Runtime carried along.
  |
  |The add-in is a COM server: register k17xl_1_0.dll once on that computer by running
  |_install.bat beside it (for every user when run as an administrator, else for the current
  |user alone), then open the add-in workbook in Excel or call the component from VBA. Excel
  |installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for Excel run
  |_install.bat as an administrator.
  |
  |2. Files to Deploy and Package
  |
  |-k17xl_1_0.dll (output from mcc)
  |-The JGraph Runtime installer, when the computer has no JGraph Runtime
  |-This readme file
  |
  |NOTE: Verify that k17xl_1_0.dll is registered on the target machine. If registration fails,
  |      run _install.bat from a command prompt and read what it prints.
  |
  |3. Definitions
  |
  |An Excel add-in is a COM DLL whose class exposes the MATLAB functions in its deployable
  |archive (embedded in the DLL, or the .ctf beside it) as methods Excel's VBA calls on the
  |JGraph Runtime; the .bas module declares a worksheet function for each, and the add-in
  |workbook (.xlam) carries that module into Excel: open it there, or add it under File > Options >
  |Add-ins, and the functions can be used in any workbook's cells.

plain2.file.GettingStarted.html	1678 chars, CRLF 0, ends with newline 1
  |<!DOCTYPE html>
  |<html>
  |    <head>
  |        <meta charset="utf-8">
  |        <title>Getting Started with the k17xl Excel Add-In</title>
  |    </head>
  |    <body>
  |        <h1>Getting Started with the k17xl Excel Add-In</h1>
  |        <p>JGraph's compiler creates Excel add-ins that can be integrated with Excel applications. You can use this guide to set up your environment.</p>
  |        <h2>Location of Installed Excel Component Files</h2>
  |        <p>View k17xl Files</p>
  |        <h2>Prerequisites</h2>
  |        <p>Install the JGraph Runtime 0.2.3 on the computer that runs Excel, or set JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to the folder of a runtime carried along.</p>
  |        <p>Complete this step only if you have not installed the JGraph Runtime while installing the add-in.</p>
  |        <h2>Register the Add-In</h2>
  |        <p>Run _install.bat beside the add-in's DLL once: as an administrator it registers the component for every user, otherwise for the current user alone. Excel installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for Excel run it as an administrator.</p>
  |        <h2>Use the Add-In in Excel</h2>
  |        <p>Open k17xl.xlam in Excel, or add it under File &gt; Options &gt; Add-ins; its worksheet functions, one per exported function, can then be used in any workbook's cells. The module itself is k17xl.bas, which can be imported into a workbook's own VBA project instead.</p>
  |        <h2>Additional Resources</h2>
  |        <p>The deployment chapter of the JGraph scripting guide (docs/jgs-scripting-guide.html) says how the add-in's functions are called from worksheet cells and from VBA, and how the add-in is distributed.</p>
  |    </body>
  |</html>
plain2.file._install.bat	880 chars, CRLF 0, ends with newline 0
  |@ECHO OFF
  |
  |:START
  |SETLOCAL
  |set FILE=k17xl_1_0.dll
  |ECHO Registering %FILE%...
  |where MWRegsvr 1>NUL 2>&1
  |if ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO USEREGSVR
  |
  |MWRegsvr %FILE%
  |IF ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO :USERONLYREG
  |GOTO :SUCCESS
  |
  |:USERONLYREG
  |ECHO.
  |SET YOURINPUT=
  |Set /P YOURINPUT=Do you want to register the component for YOU only? [Y]es or [N]o:
  |IF NOT "%YOURINPUT%"=="" SET YOURINPUT=%YOURINPUT:~0,1%
  |IF /I "%YOURINPUT%" == "Y" (
  |  ECHO Registering the component for you only...
  |    MWRegsvr /useronly %FILE%
  |  if ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO FAILURE
  |  GOTO SUCCESS
  |)
  |ECHO Did not register the component.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:USEREGSVR
  |REM if mwregsvr is not available use regsvr32
  |ECHO Using regsvr32 to register the component...
  |regsvr32 %FILE%
  |GOTO SUCCESS
  |
  |:FAILURE
  |ECHO Failed to register the component.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:SUCCESS
  |ECHO Please refer to the documentation for any additional setup steps.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:END
  |ENDLOCAL
  |ECHO.

plain2.file.readme.txt	1434 chars, CRLF 30, ends with newline 1
  |k17xl JGraph Excel Add-in 
  |
  |1. Prerequisites for Deployment 
  |
  |Verify that the JGraph Runtime (0.2.3) is installed on the computer that
  |runs Excel. If not, install it from its installer, or set JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to the folder
  |of a JGraph Runtime carried along.
  |
  |The add-in is a COM server: register k17xl_1_0.dll once on that computer by running
  |_install.bat beside it (for every user when run as an administrator, else for the current
  |user alone), then open the add-in workbook in Excel or call the component from VBA. Excel
  |installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for Excel run
  |_install.bat as an administrator.
  |
  |2. Files to Deploy and Package
  |
  |-k17xl_1_0.dll (output from mcc)
  |-The JGraph Runtime installer, when the computer has no JGraph Runtime
  |-This readme file
  |
  |NOTE: Verify that k17xl_1_0.dll is registered on the target machine. If registration fails,
  |      run _install.bat from a command prompt and read what it prints.
  |
  |3. Definitions
  |
  |An Excel add-in is a COM DLL whose class exposes the MATLAB functions in its deployable
  |archive (embedded in the DLL, or the .ctf beside it) as methods Excel's VBA calls on the
  |JGraph Runtime; the .bas module declares a worksheet function for each, and the add-in
  |workbook (.xlam) carries that module into Excel: open it there, or add it under File > Options >
  |Add-ins, and the functions can be used in any workbook's cells.

  |        <p>Open k17bas.xlam in Excel, or add it under File &gt; Options &gt; Add-ins; its worksheet functions, one per exported function, can then be used in any workbook's cells. The module itself is k17bas.bas, which can be imported into a workbook's own VBA project instead.</p>
  |        <h2>Additional Resources</h2>
  |        <p>The deployment chapter of the JGraph scripting guide (docs/jgs-scripting-guide.html) says how the add-in's functions are called from worksheet cells and from VBA, and how the add-in is distributed.</p>
  |    </body>
  |</html>

  |@ECHO OFF
  |
  |:START
  |SETLOCAL
  |set FILE=k17bas_1_0.dll
  |ECHO Registering %FILE%...
  |where MWRegsvr 1>NUL 2>&1
  |if ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO USEREGSVR
  |
  |MWRegsvr %FILE%
  |IF ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO :USERONLYREG
  |GOTO :SUCCESS
  |
  |:USERONLYREG
  |ECHO.
  |SET YOURINPUT=
  |Set /P YOURINPUT=Do you want to register the component for YOU only? [Y]es or [N]o:
  |IF NOT "%YOURINPUT%"=="" SET YOURINPUT=%YOURINPUT:~0,1%
  |IF /I "%YOURINPUT%" == "Y" (
  |  ECHO Registering the component for you only...
  |    MWRegsvr /useronly %FILE%
  |  if ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO FAILURE
  |  GOTO SUCCESS
  |)
  |ECHO Did not register the component.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:USEREGSVR
  |REM if mwregsvr is not available use regsvr32
  |ECHO Using regsvr32 to register the component...
  |regsvr32 %FILE%
  |GOTO SUCCESS
  |
  |:FAILURE
  |ECHO Failed to register the component.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:SUCCESS
  |ECHO Please refer to the documentation for any additional setup steps.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:END
  |ENDLOCAL
  |ECHO.

  |        On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |        If MCLUtil Is Nothing Then
  |            Set MCLUtil = CreateObject("JGraphComUtil.MWUtil25.2")

  |Function pack(ParamArray inputs() As Variant) As Variant
  |    Dim i As Integer
  |    Dim c As New Collection
  |    Dim result() As Variant
  |    
  |    For i = LBound(inputs) To UBound(inputs)
  |        If Not IsMissing(inputs(i)) And Not IsEmpty(inputs(i)) Then
  |            c.Add inputs(i)
  |        End If
  |    Next
  |    
  |    'Convert collection to array
  |    If c.Count > 0 Then
  |        ReDim result(0 To (c.Count - 1))
  |        For i = 1 To c.Count
  |            result(i - 1) = c.Item(i)
  |        Next
  |        pack = result
  |    Else
  |        pack = Empty
  |    End If
  |
  |End Function

bas.file.readme.txt	1537 chars, CRLF 32, ends with newline 1
  |k17bas JGraph Excel Add-in 
  |
  |1. Prerequisites for Deployment 
  |
  |Verify that the JGraph Runtime (0.2.3) is installed on the computer that
  |runs Excel. If not, install it from its installer, or set JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to the folder
  |of a JGraph Runtime carried along.
  |
  |The add-in is a COM server: register k17bas_1_0.dll once on that computer by running
  |_install.bat beside it (for every user when run as an administrator, else for the current
  |user alone), then open the add-in workbook in Excel or call the component from VBA. Excel
  |installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for Excel run
  |_install.bat as an administrator.
  |
  |2. Files to Deploy and Package
  |
  |-k17bas_1_0.dll (output from mcc)
  |-k17bas.bas (VBA module file created by mcc)
  |-k17bas.xlam (Excel add-in workbook created by mcc)
  |-The JGraph Runtime installer, when the computer has no JGraph Runtime
  |-This readme file
  |
  |NOTE: Verify that k17bas_1_0.dll is registered on the target machine. If registration fails,
  |      run _install.bat from a command prompt and read what it prints.
  |
  |3. Definitions
  |
  |An Excel add-in is a COM DLL whose class exposes the MATLAB functions in its deployable
  |archive (embedded in the DLL, or the .ctf beside it) as methods Excel's VBA calls on the
  |JGraph Runtime; the .bas module declares a worksheet function for each, and the add-in
  |workbook (.xlam) carries that module into Excel: open it there, or add it under File > Options >
  |Add-ins, and the functions can be used in any workbook's cells.

noembed.warn	Compiler:compiler:COM_WARN_PER_USER_REGISTRATION | Component "k17sep_1_0" will be registered for the current user only.  / Applications that run with full administrative privileges cannot access the component.  / To register the component for all users, run JGraph or the mcc command as Administrator.
noembed.file.GettingStarted.html	1683 chars, CRLF 0, ends with newline 1
  |<!DOCTYPE html>
  |<html>
  |    <head>
  |        <meta charset="utf-8">
  |        <title>Getting Started with the k17sep Excel Add-In</title>
  |    </head>
  |    <body>
  |        <h1>Getting Started with the k17sep Excel Add-In</h1>
  |        <p>JGraph's compiler creates Excel add-ins that can be integrated with Excel applications. You can use this guide to set up your environment.</p>
  |        <h2>Location of Installed Excel Component Files</h2>
  |        <p>View k17sep Files</p>
  |        <h2>Prerequisites</h2>
  |        <p>Install the JGraph Runtime 0.2.3 on the computer that runs Excel, or set JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to the folder of a runtime carried along.</p>
  |        <p>Complete this step only if you have not installed the JGraph Runtime while installing the add-in.</p>
  |        <h2>Register the Add-In</h2>
  |        <p>Run _install.bat beside the add-in's DLL once: as an administrator it registers the component for every user, otherwise for the current user alone. Excel installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for Excel run it as an administrator.</p>
  |        <h2>Use the Add-In in Excel</h2>
  |        <p>Open k17sep.xlam in Excel, or add it under File &gt; Options &gt; Add-ins; its worksheet functions, one per exported function, can then be used in any workbook's cells. The module itself is k17sep.bas, which can be imported into a workbook's own VBA project instead.</p>
  |        <h2>Additional Resources</h2>
  |        <p>The deployment chapter of the JGraph scripting guide (docs/jgs-scripting-guide.html) says how the add-in's functions are called from worksheet cells and from VBA, and how the add-in is distributed.</p>
  |    </body>
  |</html>
noembed.file._install.bat	881 chars, CRLF 0, ends with newline 0
  |@ECHO OFF
  |
  |:START
  |SETLOCAL
  |set FILE=k17sep_1_0.dll
  |ECHO Registering %FILE%...
  |where MWRegsvr 1>NUL 2>&1
  |if ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO USEREGSVR
  |
  |MWRegsvr %FILE%
  |IF ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO :USERONLYREG
  |GOTO :SUCCESS
  |
  |:USERONLYREG
  |ECHO.
  |SET YOURINPUT=
  |Set /P YOURINPUT=Do you want to register the component for YOU only? [Y]es or [N]o:
  |IF NOT "%YOURINPUT%"=="" SET YOURINPUT=%YOURINPUT:~0,1%
  |IF /I "%YOURINPUT%" == "Y" (
  |  ECHO Registering the component for you only...
  |    MWRegsvr /useronly %FILE%
  |  if ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO FAILURE
  |  GOTO SUCCESS
  |)
  |ECHO Did not register the component.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:USEREGSVR
  |REM if mwregsvr is not available use regsvr32
  |ECHO Using regsvr32 to register the component...
  |regsvr32 %FILE%
  |GOTO SUCCESS
  |
  |:FAILURE
  |ECHO Failed to register the component.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:SUCCESS
  |ECHO Please refer to the documentation for any additional setup steps.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:END
  |ENDLOCAL
  |ECHO.

  |Dim MCLUtil As Object
  |Dim bModuleInitialized As Boolean
  |Dim K17Sep As Object

  |Private Sub InitModule()
  |    If Not bModuleInitialized Then
  |        On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |        If MCLUtil Is Nothing Then
  |            Set MCLUtil = CreateObject("JGraphComUtil.MWUtil25.2")
  |        End If
  |        Call MCLUtil.MWInitApplication(Application)
  |        
  |        bModuleInitialized = True
  |        Exit Sub
  |Handle_Error:
  |        bModuleInitialized = False
  |    End If
  |End Sub

  |Function k17_quiet() As Variant

  |        Set K17Sep = CreateObject("k17sep.K17Sep.1_0")
  |    End If

  |    k17_quiet = Empty

  |Function k17_many(Optional a As Variant, Optional b As Variant, Optional c As Variant) As  _
  |    Variant
  |
  |    Dim s, d, p As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    Call InitModule()
  |
  |    If K17Sep Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17Sep = CreateObject("k17sep.K17Sep.1_0")
  |    End If
  |
  |    ' By default, MATLAB Excel Add-in returns just one output for MATLAB functions 
  |    ' that have more than one output.    
  |
  |    Call K17Sep.k17_many(3, s, d, p, a, b, c)
  |    k17_many = s
  |
  |    ' If you want to use all the return values, you can use the following 
  |    ' code as a guideline. To use this form, call the function from Excel cell,
  |    ' highlight the cells which will receive the outputs and hit Ctrl+Shift+Enter.
  |    '
  |    ' Call K17Sep.k17_many(3, s, d, p, a, b, c)
  |    ' k17_many = Array(s, d, p)
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_many = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function

  |Function pack(ParamArray inputs() As Variant) As Variant
  |    Dim i As Integer
  |    Dim c As New Collection
  |    Dim result() As Variant
  |    
  |    For i = LBound(inputs) To UBound(inputs)
  |        If Not IsMissing(inputs(i)) And Not IsEmpty(inputs(i)) Then
  |            c.Add inputs(i)
  |        End If
  |    Next
  |    
  |    'Convert collection to array
  |    If c.Count > 0 Then
  |        ReDim result(0 To (c.Count - 1))
  |        For i = 1 To c.Count
  |            result(i - 1) = c.Item(i)
  |        Next
  |        pack = result
  |    Else
  |        pack = Empty
  |    End If
  |
  |End Function

noembed.file.readme.txt	1578 chars, CRLF 33, ends with newline 1
  |k17sep JGraph Excel Add-in 
  |
  |1. Prerequisites for Deployment 
  |
  |Verify that the JGraph Runtime (0.2.3) is installed on the computer that
  |runs Excel. If not, install it from its installer, or set JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to the folder
  |of a JGraph Runtime carried along.
  |
  |The add-in is a COM server: register k17sep_1_0.dll once on that computer by running
  |_install.bat beside it (for every user when run as an administrator, else for the current
  |user alone), then open the add-in workbook in Excel or call the component from VBA. Excel
  |installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for Excel run
  |_install.bat as an administrator.
  |
  |2. Files to Deploy and Package
  |
  |-k17sep.ctf (component technology file)
  |-k17sep_1_0.dll (output from mcc)
  |-k17sep.bas (VBA module file created by mcc)
  |-k17sep.xlam (Excel add-in workbook created by mcc)
  |-The JGraph Runtime installer, when the computer has no JGraph Runtime
  |-This readme file
  |
  |NOTE: Verify that k17sep_1_0.dll is registered on the target machine. If registration fails,
  |      run _install.bat from a command prompt and read what it prints.
  |
  |3. Definitions
  |
  |An Excel add-in is a COM DLL whose class exposes the MATLAB functions in its deployable
  |archive (embedded in the DLL, or the .ctf beside it) as methods Excel's VBA calls on the
  |JGraph Runtime; the .bas module declares a worksheet function for each, and the add-in
  |workbook (.xlam) carries that module into Excel: open it there, or add it under File > Options >
  |Add-ins, and the functions can be used in any workbook's cells.

debug.file.GettingStarted.html	1683 chars, CRLF 0, ends with newline 1
  |<!DOCTYPE html>
  |<html>
  |    <head>
  |        <meta charset="utf-8">
  |        <title>Getting Started with the k17dbg Excel Add-In</title>
  |    </head>
  |    <body>
  |        <h1>Getting Started with the k17dbg Excel Add-In</h1>
  |        <p>JGraph's compiler creates Excel add-ins that can be integrated with Excel applications. You can use this guide to set up your environment.</p>
  |        <h2>Location of Installed Excel Component Files</h2>
  |        <p>View k17dbg Files</p>
  |        <h2>Prerequisites</h2>
  |        <p>Install the JGraph Runtime 0.2.3 on the computer that runs Excel, or set JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to the folder of a runtime carried along.</p>
  |        <p>Complete this step only if you have not installed the JGraph Runtime while installing the add-in.</p>
  |        <h2>Register the Add-In</h2>
  |        <p>Run _install.bat beside the add-in's DLL once: as an administrator it registers the component for every user, otherwise for the current user alone. Excel installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for Excel run it as an administrator.</p>
  |        <h2>Use the Add-In in Excel</h2>
  |        <p>Open k17dbg.xlam in Excel, or add it under File &gt; Options &gt; Add-ins; its worksheet functions, one per exported function, can then be used in any workbook's cells. The module itself is k17dbg.bas, which can be imported into a workbook's own VBA project instead.</p>
  |        <h2>Additional Resources</h2>
  |        <p>The deployment chapter of the JGraph scripting guide (docs/jgs-scripting-guide.html) says how the add-in's functions are called from worksheet cells and from VBA, and how the add-in is distributed.</p>
  |    </body>
  |</html>
debug.file._install.bat	881 chars, CRLF 0, ends with newline 0
  |@ECHO OFF
  |
  |:START
  |SETLOCAL
  |set FILE=k17dbg_1_0.dll
  |ECHO Registering %FILE%...
  |where MWRegsvr 1>NUL 2>&1
  |if ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO USEREGSVR
  |
  |MWRegsvr %FILE%
  |IF ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO :USERONLYREG
  |GOTO :SUCCESS
  |
  |:USERONLYREG
  |ECHO.
  |SET YOURINPUT=
  |Set /P YOURINPUT=Do you want to register the component for YOU only? [Y]es or [N]o:
  |IF NOT "%YOURINPUT%"=="" SET YOURINPUT=%YOURINPUT:~0,1%
  |IF /I "%YOURINPUT%" == "Y" (
  |  ECHO Registering the component for you only...
  |    MWRegsvr /useronly %FILE%
  |  if ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO FAILURE
  |  GOTO SUCCESS
  |)
  |ECHO Did not register the component.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:USEREGSVR
  |REM if mwregsvr is not available use regsvr32
  |ECHO Using regsvr32 to register the component...
  |regsvr32 %FILE%
  |GOTO SUCCESS
  |
  |:FAILURE
  |ECHO Failed to register the component.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:SUCCESS
  |ECHO Please refer to the documentation for any additional setup steps.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:END
  |ENDLOCAL
  |ECHO.

debug.file.readme.txt	1438 chars, CRLF 30, ends with newline 1
  |k17dbg JGraph Excel Add-in 
  |
  |1. Prerequisites for Deployment 
  |
  |Verify that the JGraph Runtime (0.2.3) is installed on the computer that
  |runs Excel. If not, install it from its installer, or set JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to the folder
  |of a JGraph Runtime carried along.
  |
  |The add-in is a COM server: register k17dbg_1_0.dll once on that computer by running
  |_install.bat beside it (for every user when run as an administrator, else for the current
  |user alone), then open the add-in workbook in Excel or call the component from VBA. Excel
  |installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for Excel run
  |_install.bat as an administrator.
  |
  |2. Files to Deploy and Package
  |
  |-k17dbg_1_0.dll (output from mcc)
  |-The JGraph Runtime installer, when the computer has no JGraph Runtime
  |-This readme file
  |
  |NOTE: Verify that k17dbg_1_0.dll is registered on the target machine. If registration fails,
  |      run _install.bat from a command prompt and read what it prints.
  |
  |3. Definitions
  |
  |An Excel add-in is a COM DLL whose class exposes the MATLAB functions in its deployable
  |archive (embedded in the DLL, or the .ctf beside it) as methods Excel's VBA calls on the
  |JGraph Runtime; the .bas module declares a worksheet function for each, and the add-in
  |workbook (.xlam) carries that module into Excel: open it there, or add it under File > Options >
  |Add-ins, and the functions can be used in any workbook's cells.

v05.file.readme.txt	1438 chars, CRLF 30, ends with newline 1
  |k17v05 JGraph Excel Add-in 
  |
  |1. Prerequisites for Deployment 
  |
  |Verify that the JGraph Runtime (0.2.3) is installed on the computer that
  |runs Excel. If not, install it from its installer, or set JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to the folder
  |of a JGraph Runtime carried along.
  |
  |The add-in is a COM server: register k17v05_1_0.dll once on that computer by running
  |_install.bat beside it (for every user when run as an administrator, else for the current
  |user alone), then open the add-in workbook in Excel or call the component from VBA. Excel
  |installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for Excel run
  |_install.bat as an administrator.
  |
  |2. Files to Deploy and Package
  |
  |-k17v05_1_0.dll (output from mcc)
  |-The JGraph Runtime installer, when the computer has no JGraph Runtime
  |-This readme file
  |
  |NOTE: Verify that k17v05_1_0.dll is registered on the target machine. If registration fails,
  |      run _install.bat from a command prompt and read what it prints.
  |
  |3. Definitions
  |
  |An Excel add-in is a COM DLL whose class exposes the MATLAB functions in its deployable
  |archive (embedded in the DLL, or the .ctf beside it) as methods Excel's VBA calls on the
  |JGraph Runtime; the .bas module declares a worksheet function for each, and the add-in
  |workbook (.xlam) carries that module into Excel: open it there, or add it under File > Options >
  |Add-ins, and the functions can be used in any workbook's cells.

v3456.file.GettingStarted.html	1693 chars, CRLF 0, ends with newline 1
  |<!DOCTYPE html>
  |<html>
  |    <head>
  |        <meta charset="utf-8">
  |        <title>Getting Started with the k17v3456 Excel Add-In</title>
  |    </head>
  |    <body>
  |        <h1>Getting Started with the k17v3456 Excel Add-In</h1>
  |        <p>JGraph's compiler creates Excel add-ins that can be integrated with Excel applications. You can use this guide to set up your environment.</p>
  |        <h2>Location of Installed Excel Component Files</h2>
  |        <p>View k17v3456 Files</p>
  |        <h2>Prerequisites</h2>
  |        <p>Install the JGraph Runtime 0.2.3 on the computer that runs Excel, or set JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to the folder of a runtime carried along.</p>
  |        <p>Complete this step only if you have not installed the JGraph Runtime while installing the add-in.</p>
  |        <h2>Register the Add-In</h2>
  |        <p>Run _install.bat beside the add-in's DLL once: as an administrator it registers the component for every user, otherwise for the current user alone. Excel installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for Excel run it as an administrator.</p>
  |        <h2>Use the Add-In in Excel</h2>
  |        <p>Open k17v3456.xlam in Excel, or add it under File &gt; Options &gt; Add-ins; its worksheet functions, one per exported function, can then be used in any workbook's cells. The module itself is k17v3456.bas, which can be imported into a workbook's own VBA project instead.</p>
  |        <h2>Additional Resources</h2>
  |        <p>The deployment chapter of the JGraph scripting guide (docs/jgs-scripting-guide.html) says how the add-in's functions are called from worksheet cells and from VBA, and how the add-in is distributed.</p>
  |    </body>
  |</html>
v3456.file._install.bat	883 chars, CRLF 0, ends with newline 0
  |@ECHO OFF
  |
  |:START
  |SETLOCAL
  |set FILE=k17v3456_3_4.dll
  |ECHO Registering %FILE%...
  |where MWRegsvr 1>NUL 2>&1
  |if ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO USEREGSVR
  |
  |MWRegsvr %FILE%
  |IF ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO :USERONLYREG
  |GOTO :SUCCESS
  |
  |:USERONLYREG
  |ECHO.
  |SET YOURINPUT=
  |Set /P YOURINPUT=Do you want to register the component for YOU only? [Y]es or [N]o:
  |IF NOT "%YOURINPUT%"=="" SET YOURINPUT=%YOURINPUT:~0,1%
  |IF /I "%YOURINPUT%" == "Y" (
  |  ECHO Registering the component for you only...
  |    MWRegsvr /useronly %FILE%
  |  if ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO FAILURE
  |  GOTO SUCCESS
  |)
  |ECHO Did not register the component.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:USEREGSVR
  |REM if mwregsvr is not available use regsvr32
  |ECHO Using regsvr32 to register the component...
  |regsvr32 %FILE%
  |GOTO SUCCESS
  |
  |:FAILURE
  |ECHO Failed to register the component.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:SUCCESS
  |ECHO Please refer to the documentation for any additional setup steps.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:END
  |ENDLOCAL
  |ECHO.

v3456.file.k17v3456.bas	2237 chars, CRLF 88, ends with newline 1
  |Dim MCLUtil As Object
  |Dim bModuleInitialized As Boolean
  |Dim K17V3456 As Object 
  |
  |Private Sub InitModule()
  |    If Not bModuleInitialized Then
  |        On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |        If MCLUtil Is Nothing Then
  |            Set MCLUtil = CreateObject("JGraphComUtil.MWUtil25.2")
  |        End If
  |        Call MCLUtil.MWInitApplication(Application)
  |        
  |        bModuleInitialized = True
  |        Exit Sub
  |Handle_Error:
  |        bModuleInitialized = False
  |    End If
  |End Sub
  |
  |Function k17_none() As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    Call InitModule()
  |
  |    If K17V3456 Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17V3456 = CreateObject("k17v3456.K17V3456.3_4")
  |    End If
  |
  |    Call K17V3456.k17_none()
  |    k17_none = Empty
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_none = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |
  |Function k17_one(Optional x As Variant) As Variant
  |
  |    Dim y As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    Call InitModule()
  |
  |    If K17V3456 Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17V3456 = CreateObject("k17v3456.K17V3456.3_4")
  |    End If
  |
  |    Call K17V3456.k17_one(1, y, x)
  |    k17_one = y
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_one = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |
  |Function k17_many(Optional a As Variant, Optional b As Variant, Optional c As Variant) As  _
  |    Variant
  |
  |    Dim s, d, p As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    Call InitModule()
  |
  |    If K17V3456 Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17V3456 = CreateObject("k17v3456.K17V3456.3_4")
  |    End If
  |
  |    ' By default, MATLAB Excel Add-in returns just one output for MATLAB functions 
  |    ' that have more than one output.    
  |
  |    Call K17V3456.k17_many(3, s, d, p, a, b, c)
  |    k17_many = s
  |
  |    ' If you want to use all the return values, you can use the following 
  |    ' code as a guideline. To use this form, call the function from Excel cell,
  |    ' highlight the cells which will receive the outputs and hit Ctrl+Shift+Enter.
  |    '
  |    ' Call K17V3456.k17_many(3, s, d, p, a, b, c)
  |    ' k17_many = Array(s, d, p)
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_many = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |

vzero.file.readme.txt	1434 chars, CRLF 30, ends with newline 1
  |k17vz JGraph Excel Add-in 
  |
  |1. Prerequisites for Deployment 
  |
  |Verify that the JGraph Runtime (0.2.3) is installed on the computer that
  |runs Excel. If not, install it from its installer, or set JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to the folder
  |of a JGraph Runtime carried along.
  |
  |The add-in is a COM server: register k17vz_1_0.dll once on that computer by running
  |_install.bat beside it (for every user when run as an administrator, else for the current
  |user alone), then open the add-in workbook in Excel or call the component from VBA. Excel
  |installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for Excel run
  |_install.bat as an administrator.
  |
  |2. Files to Deploy and Package
  |
  |-k17vz_1_0.dll (output from mcc)
  |-The JGraph Runtime installer, when the computer has no JGraph Runtime
  |-This readme file
  |
  |NOTE: Verify that k17vz_1_0.dll is registered on the target machine. If registration fails,
  |      run _install.bat from a command prompt and read what it prints.
  |
  |3. Definitions
  |
  |An Excel add-in is a COM DLL whose class exposes the MATLAB functions in its deployable
  |archive (embedded in the DLL, or the .ctf beside it) as methods Excel's VBA calls on the
  |JGraph Runtime; the .bas module declares a worksheet function for each, and the add-in
  |workbook (.xlam) carries that module into Excel: open it there, or add it under File > Options >
  |Add-ins, and the functions can be used in any workbook's cells.

defaults.file.GettingStarted.html	1688 chars, CRLF 0, ends with newline 1
  |<!DOCTYPE html>
  |<html>
  |    <head>
  |        <meta charset="utf-8">
  |        <title>Getting Started with the k17_one Excel Add-In</title>
  |    </head>
  |    <body>
  |        <h1>Getting Started with the k17_one Excel Add-In</h1>
  |        <p>JGraph's compiler creates Excel add-ins that can be integrated with Excel applications. You can use this guide to set up your environment.</p>
  |        <h2>Location of Installed Excel Component Files</h2>
  |        <p>View k17_one Files</p>
  |        <h2>Prerequisites</h2>
  |        <p>Install the JGraph Runtime 0.2.3 on the computer that runs Excel, or set JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to the folder of a runtime carried along.</p>
  |        <p>Complete this step only if you have not installed the JGraph Runtime while installing the add-in.</p>
  |        <h2>Register the Add-In</h2>
  |        <p>Run _install.bat beside the add-in's DLL once: as an administrator it registers the component for every user, otherwise for the current user alone. Excel installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for Excel run it as an administrator.</p>
  |        <h2>Use the Add-In in Excel</h2>
  |        <p>Open k17_one.xlam in Excel, or add it under File &gt; Options &gt; Add-ins; its worksheet functions, one per exported function, can then be used in any workbook's cells. The module itself is k17_one.bas, which can be imported into a workbook's own VBA project instead.</p>
  |        <h2>Additional Resources</h2>
  |        <p>The deployment chapter of the JGraph scripting guide (docs/jgs-scripting-guide.html) says how the add-in's functions are called from worksheet cells and from VBA, and how the add-in is distributed.</p>
  |    </body>
  |</html>
defaults.file._install.bat	882 chars, CRLF 0, ends with newline 0
  |@ECHO OFF
  |
  |:START
  |SETLOCAL
  |set FILE=k17_one_1_0.dll
  |ECHO Registering %FILE%...
  |where MWRegsvr 1>NUL 2>&1
  |if ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO USEREGSVR
  |
  |MWRegsvr %FILE%
  |IF ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO :USERONLYREG
  |GOTO :SUCCESS
  |
  |:USERONLYREG
  |ECHO.
  |SET YOURINPUT=
  |Set /P YOURINPUT=Do you want to register the component for YOU only? [Y]es or [N]o:
  |IF NOT "%YOURINPUT%"=="" SET YOURINPUT=%YOURINPUT:~0,1%
  |IF /I "%YOURINPUT%" == "Y" (
  |  ECHO Registering the component for you only...
  |    MWRegsvr /useronly %FILE%
  |  if ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO FAILURE
  |  GOTO SUCCESS
  |)
  |ECHO Did not register the component.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:USEREGSVR
  |REM if mwregsvr is not available use regsvr32
  |ECHO Using regsvr32 to register the component...
  |regsvr32 %FILE%
  |GOTO SUCCESS
  |
  |:FAILURE
  |ECHO Failed to register the component.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:SUCCESS
  |ECHO Please refer to the documentation for any additional setup steps.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:END
  |ENDLOCAL
  |ECHO.

pfile.file.GettingStarted.html	1673 chars, CRLF 0, ends with newline 1
  |<!DOCTYPE html>
  |<html>
  |    <head>
  |        <meta charset="utf-8">
  |        <title>Getting Started with the k17p Excel Add-In</title>
  |    </head>
  |    <body>
  |        <h1>Getting Started with the k17p Excel Add-In</h1>
  |        <p>JGraph's compiler creates Excel add-ins that can be integrated with Excel applications. You can use this guide to set up your environment.</p>
  |        <h2>Location of Installed Excel Component Files</h2>
  |        <p>View k17p Files</p>
  |        <h2>Prerequisites</h2>
  |        <p>Install the JGraph Runtime 0.2.3 on the computer that runs Excel, or set JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to the folder of a runtime carried along.</p>
  |        <p>Complete this step only if you have not installed the JGraph Runtime while installing the add-in.</p>
  |        <h2>Register the Add-In</h2>
  |        <p>Run _install.bat beside the add-in's DLL once: as an administrator it registers the component for every user, otherwise for the current user alone. Excel installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for Excel run it as an administrator.</p>
  |        <h2>Use the Add-In in Excel</h2>
  |        <p>Open k17p.xlam in Excel, or add it under File &gt; Options &gt; Add-ins; its worksheet functions, one per exported function, can then be used in any workbook's cells. The module itself is k17p.bas, which can be imported into a workbook's own VBA project instead.</p>
  |        <h2>Additional Resources</h2>
  |        <p>The deployment chapter of the JGraph scripting guide (docs/jgs-scripting-guide.html) says how the add-in's functions are called from worksheet cells and from VBA, and how the add-in is distributed.</p>
  |    </body>
  |</html>
pfile.file._install.bat	879 chars, CRLF 0, ends with newline 0
  |@ECHO OFF
  |
  |:START
  |SETLOCAL
  |set FILE=k17p_1_0.dll
  |ECHO Registering %FILE%...
  |where MWRegsvr 1>NUL 2>&1
  |if ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO USEREGSVR
  |
  |MWRegsvr %FILE%
  |IF ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO :USERONLYREG
  |GOTO :SUCCESS
  |
  |:USERONLYREG
  |ECHO.
  |SET YOURINPUT=
  |Set /P YOURINPUT=Do you want to register the component for YOU only? [Y]es or [N]o:
  |IF NOT "%YOURINPUT%"=="" SET YOURINPUT=%YOURINPUT:~0,1%
  |IF /I "%YOURINPUT%" == "Y" (
  |  ECHO Registering the component for you only...
  |    MWRegsvr /useronly %FILE%
  |  if ERRORLEVEL 1 GOTO FAILURE
  |  GOTO SUCCESS
  |)
  |ECHO Did not register the component.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:USEREGSVR
  |REM if mwregsvr is not available use regsvr32
  |ECHO Using regsvr32 to register the component...
  |regsvr32 %FILE%
  |GOTO SUCCESS
  |
  |:FAILURE
  |ECHO Failed to register the component.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:SUCCESS
  |ECHO Please refer to the documentation for any additional setup steps.
  |GOTO END
  |
  |:END
  |ENDLOCAL
  |ECHO.

pfile.file.k17p.bas	1290 chars, CRLF 59, ends with newline 1
  |Dim MCLUtil As Object
  |Dim bModuleInitialized As Boolean
  |Dim K17P As Object 
  |
  |Private Sub InitModule()
  |    If Not bModuleInitialized Then
  |        On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |        If MCLUtil Is Nothing Then
  |            Set MCLUtil = CreateObject("JGraphComUtil.MWUtil25.2")
  |        End If
  |        Call MCLUtil.MWInitApplication(Application)
  |        
  |        bModuleInitialized = True
  |        Exit Sub
  |Handle_Error:
  |        bModuleInitialized = False
  |    End If
  |End Sub
  |
  |Function k17_pfun(Optional i0 As Variant) As Variant
  |
  |    Dim o0 As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    Call InitModule()
  |
  |    If K17P Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17P = CreateObject("k17p.K17P.1_0")
  |    End If
  |
  |    Call K17P.k17_pfun(1, o0, i0)
  |    k17_pfun = o0
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_pfun = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |
  |Function k17_one(Optional x As Variant) As Variant
  |
  |    Dim y As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    Call InitModule()
  |
  |    If K17P Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17P = CreateObject("k17p.K17P.1_0")
  |    End If
  |
  |    Call K17P.k17_one(1, y, x)
  |    k17_one = y
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_one = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |

pfile.file.readme.txt	1525 chars, CRLF 32, ends with newline 1
  |k17p JGraph Excel Add-in 
  |
  |1. Prerequisites for Deployment 
  |
  |Verify that the JGraph Runtime (0.2.3) is installed on the computer that
  |runs Excel. If not, install it from its installer, or set JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to the folder
  |of a JGraph Runtime carried along.
  |
  |The add-in is a COM server: register k17p_1_0.dll once on that computer by running
  |_install.bat beside it (for every user when run as an administrator, else for the current
  |user alone), then open the add-in workbook in Excel or call the component from VBA. Excel
  |installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for Excel run
  |_install.bat as an administrator.
  |
  |2. Files to Deploy and Package
  |
  |-k17p_1_0.dll (output from mcc)
  |-k17p.bas (VBA module file created by mcc)
  |-k17p.xlam (Excel add-in workbook created by mcc)
  |-The JGraph Runtime installer, when the computer has no JGraph Runtime
  |-This readme file
  |
  |NOTE: Verify that k17p_1_0.dll is registered on the target machine. If registration fails,
  |      run _install.bat from a command prompt and read what it prints.
  |
  |3. Definitions
  |
  |An Excel add-in is a COM DLL whose class exposes the MATLAB functions in its deployable
  |archive (embedded in the DLL, or the .ctf beside it) as methods Excel's VBA calls on the
  |JGraph Runtime; the .bas module declares a worksheet function for each, and the add-in
  |workbook (.xlam) carries that module into Excel: open it there, or add it under File > Options >
  |Add-ins, and the functions can be used in any workbook's cells.

mcc_default.file.k17m.bas	885 chars, CRLF 39, ends with newline 1
  |Dim MCLUtil As Object
  |Dim bModuleInitialized As Boolean
  |Dim K17M As Object 
  |
  |Private Sub InitModule()
  |    If Not bModuleInitialized Then
  |        On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |        If MCLUtil Is Nothing Then
  |            Set MCLUtil = CreateObject("JGraphComUtil.MWUtil25.2")
  |        End If
  |        Call MCLUtil.MWInitApplication(Application)
  |        
  |        bModuleInitialized = True
  |        Exit Sub
  |Handle_Error:
  |        bModuleInitialized = False
  |    End If
  |End Sub
  |
  |Function k17_one(Optional x As Variant) As Variant
  |
  |    Dim y As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    Call InitModule()
  |
  |    If K17M Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17M = CreateObject("k17m.K17M.1_0")
  |    End If
  |
  |    Call K17M.k17_one(1, y, x)
  |    k17_one = y
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_one = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |

mcc_default.file.readme.txt	1525 chars, CRLF 32, ends with newline 1
  |k17m JGraph Excel Add-in 
  |
  |1. Prerequisites for Deployment 
  |
  |Verify that the JGraph Runtime (0.2.3) is installed on the computer that
  |runs Excel. If not, install it from its installer, or set JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to the folder
  |of a JGraph Runtime carried along.
  |
  |The add-in is a COM server: register k17m_1_0.dll once on that computer by running
  |_install.bat beside it (for every user when run as an administrator, else for the current
  |user alone), then open the add-in workbook in Excel or call the component from VBA. Excel
  |installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for Excel run
  |_install.bat as an administrator.
  |
  |2. Files to Deploy and Package
  |
  |-k17m_1_0.dll (output from mcc)
  |-k17m.bas (VBA module file created by mcc)
  |-k17m.xlam (Excel add-in workbook created by mcc)
  |-The JGraph Runtime installer, when the computer has no JGraph Runtime
  |-This readme file
  |
  |NOTE: Verify that k17m_1_0.dll is registered on the target machine. If registration fails,
  |      run _install.bat from a command prompt and read what it prints.
  |
  |3. Definitions
  |
  |An Excel add-in is a COM DLL whose class exposes the MATLAB functions in its deployable
  |archive (embedded in the DLL, or the .ctf beside it) as methods Excel's VBA calls on the
  |JGraph Runtime; the .bas module declares a worksheet function for each, and the add-in
  |workbook (.xlam) carries that module into Excel: open it there, or add it under File > Options >
  |Add-ins, and the functions can be used in any workbook's cells.
mcc_default.file.requiredMCRProducts.txt	6 chars, CRLF 0, ends with newline 0

fromresults.file.readme.txt	2165 chars, CRLF 40, ends with newline 1
  |k17mps Deployable Archive with Excel Integration
  |
  |Before you can use the functions packaged by the compiler, you need to do the following:
  |
  |* Deploy the server archive (the .ctf compiler.build.productionServerArchive wrote) to a jgraph-mps instance.
  |* Install the Excel client on the client machine.
  |* Configure the client with the address of the server instance.
  |
  |1. Deploy the server archive
  |
  |Copy the archive into the instance's auto_deploy folder; jgraph-mps deploys it as it starts or
  |while it runs (jgraph-mps run <instance>). The instance needs no MATLAB Runtime: it is a JGraph
  |Production Server, which carries its own engine.
  |
  |2. Install the Excel client
  |
  |2.1 Using the installer
  |compiler.package.excelClientForProductionServer writes an installer that copies the client's
  |files, registers k17mps.dll and JGraph's server configuration add-in for the
  |current user (for every user when run as an administrator), and asks for the server's address.
  |
  |2.2 Manual installation
  |In the output folder, you will find the following files:
  |
  |* `k17mps.dll` - the client, a COM automation add-in whose functions call the server
  |* `k17mps.bas` - VBA module file created for the add-in
  |* `k17mps.xlam` - Excel add-in workbook holding that module
  |* `jgxlconfig.dll` - JGraph's server configuration add-in (beside JGraph's launchers), which the installer carries
  |
  |On the client machine, register the client once with `regsvr32 k17mps.dll` (for the current user
  |when not elevated), then open k17mps.xlam in Excel or import k17mps.bas into a workbook's
  |Visual Basic project. Excel installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for
  |Excel register as an administrator.
  |
  |Set the server's address with the Configure button JGraph's server configuration add-in puts on
  |Excel's ribbon, or write it under HKEY_CURRENT_USER\Software\JGraph\Excel Client\k17mps:
  |ServerURL (http://host:port/<archive>), timeoutSeconds and responseSizeLimit (megabytes).
  |
  |The client needs no JGraph Runtime: it talks to the server over HTTP in MATLAB Production Server's
  |protobuf format, so a MATLAB Production Server instance serves it too.

fromfiles.file.readme.txt	2174 chars, CRLF 40, ends with newline 1
  |k17mpsf Deployable Archive with Excel Integration
  |
  |Before you can use the functions packaged by the compiler, you need to do the following:
  |
  |* Deploy the server archive (the .ctf compiler.build.productionServerArchive wrote) to a jgraph-mps instance.
  |* Install the Excel client on the client machine.
  |* Configure the client with the address of the server instance.
  |
  |1. Deploy the server archive
  |
  |Copy the archive into the instance's auto_deploy folder; jgraph-mps deploys it as it starts or
  |while it runs (jgraph-mps run <instance>). The instance needs no MATLAB Runtime: it is a JGraph
  |Production Server, which carries its own engine.
  |
  |2. Install the Excel client
  |
  |2.1 Using the installer
  |compiler.package.excelClientForProductionServer writes an installer that copies the client's
  |files, registers k17mpsf.dll and JGraph's server configuration add-in for the
  |current user (for every user when run as an administrator), and asks for the server's address.
  |
  |2.2 Manual installation
  |In the output folder, you will find the following files:
  |
  |* `k17mpsf.dll` - the client, a COM automation add-in whose functions call the server
  |* `k17mpsf.bas` - VBA module file created for the add-in
  |* `k17mpsf.xlam` - Excel add-in workbook holding that module
  |* `jgxlconfig.dll` - JGraph's server configuration add-in (beside JGraph's launchers), which the installer carries
  |
  |On the client machine, register the client once with `regsvr32 k17mpsf.dll` (for the current user
  |when not elevated), then open k17mpsf.xlam in Excel or import k17mpsf.bas into a workbook's
  |Visual Basic project. Excel installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for
  |Excel register as an administrator.
  |
  |Set the server's address with the Configure button JGraph's server configuration add-in puts on
  |Excel's ribbon, or write it under HKEY_CURRENT_USER\Software\JGraph\Excel Client\k17mpsf:
  |ServerURL (http://host:port/<archive>), timeoutSeconds and responseSizeLimit (megabytes).
  |
  |The client needs no JGraph Runtime: it talks to the server over HTTP in MATLAB Production Server's
  |protobuf format, so a MATLAB Production Server instance serves it too.

  |    k17_plot = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |
  |Function k17_h1() As Variant
  |
  |    Dim t As Variant
  |

  |

  |        Set k17_quietClass = CreateObject("k17_quiet.k17_quietClass")

  |End Function

cdefaults.file.readme.txt	2192 chars, CRLF 40, ends with newline 1
  |k17_quiet Deployable Archive with Excel Integration
  |
  |Before you can use the functions packaged by the compiler, you need to do the following:
  |
  |* Deploy the server archive (the .ctf compiler.build.productionServerArchive wrote) to a jgraph-mps instance.
  |* Install the Excel client on the client machine.
  |* Configure the client with the address of the server instance.
  |
  |1. Deploy the server archive
  |
  |Copy the archive into the instance's auto_deploy folder; jgraph-mps deploys it as it starts or
  |while it runs (jgraph-mps run <instance>). The instance needs no MATLAB Runtime: it is a JGraph
  |Production Server, which carries its own engine.
  |
  |2. Install the Excel client
  |
  |2.1 Using the installer
  |compiler.package.excelClientForProductionServer writes an installer that copies the client's
  |files, registers k17_quiet.dll and JGraph's server configuration add-in for the
  |current user (for every user when run as an administrator), and asks for the server's address.
  |
  |2.2 Manual installation
  |In the output folder, you will find the following files:
  |
  |* `k17_quiet.dll` - the client, a COM automation add-in whose functions call the server
  |* `k17_quiet.bas` - VBA module file created for the add-in
  |* `k17_quiet.xlam` - Excel add-in workbook holding that module
  |* `jgxlconfig.dll` - JGraph's server configuration add-in (beside JGraph's launchers), which the installer carries
  |
  |On the client machine, register the client once with `regsvr32 k17_quiet.dll` (for the current user
  |when not elevated), then open k17_quiet.xlam in Excel or import k17_quiet.bas into a workbook's
  |Visual Basic project. Excel installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for
  |Excel register as an administrator.
  |
  |Set the server's address with the Configure button JGraph's server configuration add-in puts on
  |Excel's ribbon, or write it under HKEY_CURRENT_USER\Software\JGraph\Excel Client\k17_quiet:
  |ServerURL (http://host:port/<archive>), timeoutSeconds and responseSizeLimit (megabytes).
  |
  |The client needs no JGraph Runtime: it talks to the server over HTTP in MATLAB Production Server's
  |protobuf format, so a MATLAB Production Server instance serves it too.

  |Dim K17Cnb As Object 
  |
  |
  |Function k17_none() As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    If K17Cnb Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17Cnb = CreateObject("k17cnb.K17Cnb")
  |    End If
  |
  |    Call K17Cnb.k17_none()
  |    k17_none = Empty
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_none = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |
  |Function k17_one(Optional x As Variant) As Variant
  |
  |    Dim y As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    If K17Cnb Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17Cnb = CreateObject("k17cnb.K17Cnb")
  |    End If
  |
  |    Call K17Cnb.k17_one(1, y, x)
  |    k17_one = y
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_one = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |
  |Function k17_many(Optional a As Variant, Optional b As Variant, Optional c As Variant) As  _
  |                  Variant
  |
  |    Dim s, d, p As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    If K17Cnb Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17Cnb = CreateObject("k17cnb.K17Cnb")
  |    End If
  |
  |    ' By default, MATLAB Excel Add-in returns just one output for MATLAB functions 
  |    ' that have more than one output.    
  |
  |    Call K17Cnb.k17_many(3, s, d, p, a, b, c)
  |    k17_many = s
  |
  |    ' If you want to use all the return values, you can use the following 
  |    ' code as a guideline. To use this form, call the function from Excel cell,
  |    ' highlight the cells which will receive the outputs and hit Ctrl+Shift+Enter.
  |    '
  |    ' Call K17Cnb.k17_many(3, s, d, p, a, b, c)
  |    ' k17_many = Array(s, d, p)
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_many = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |

cv2.file.k17cv2.bas	1666 chars, CRLF 66, ends with newline 1
  |Dim K17Cv2 As Object 
  |
  |
  |Function k17_none() As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    If K17Cv2 Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17Cv2 = CreateObject("k17cv2.K17Cv2")
  |    End If
  |
  |    Call K17Cv2.k17_none()
  |    k17_none = Empty
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_none = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |
  |Function k17_one(Optional x As Variant) As Variant
  |
  |    Dim y As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    If K17Cv2 Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17Cv2 = CreateObject("k17cv2.K17Cv2")
  |    End If
  |
  |    Call K17Cv2.k17_one(1, y, x)
  |    k17_one = y
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_one = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |
  |Function k17_many(Optional a As Variant, Optional b As Variant, Optional c As Variant) As  _
  |                  Variant
  |
  |    Dim s, d, p As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    If K17Cv2 Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17Cv2 = CreateObject("k17cv2.K17Cv2")
  |    End If
  |
  |    ' By default, MATLAB Excel Add-in returns just one output for MATLAB functions 
  |    ' that have more than one output.    
  |
  |    Call K17Cv2.k17_many(3, s, d, p, a, b, c)
  |    k17_many = s
  |
  |    ' If you want to use all the return values, you can use the following 
  |    ' code as a guideline. To use this form, call the function from Excel cell,
  |    ' highlight the cells which will receive the outputs and hit Ctrl+Shift+Enter.
  |    '
  |    ' Call K17Cv2.k17_many(3, s, d, p, a, b, c)
  |    ' k17_many = Array(s, d, p)
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_many = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |
cv2.file.readme.txt	2165 chars, CRLF 40, ends with newline 1
  |k17cv2 Deployable Archive with Excel Integration
  |
  |Before you can use the functions packaged by the compiler, you need to do the following:
  |
  |* Deploy the server archive (the .ctf compiler.build.productionServerArchive wrote) to a jgraph-mps instance.
  |* Install the Excel client on the client machine.
  |* Configure the client with the address of the server instance.
  |
  |1. Deploy the server archive
  |
  |Copy the archive into the instance's auto_deploy folder; jgraph-mps deploys it as it starts or
  |while it runs (jgraph-mps run <instance>). The instance needs no MATLAB Runtime: it is a JGraph
  |Production Server, which carries its own engine.
  |
  |2. Install the Excel client
  |
  |2.1 Using the installer
  |compiler.package.excelClientForProductionServer writes an installer that copies the client's
  |files, registers k17cv2.dll and JGraph's server configuration add-in for the
  |current user (for every user when run as an administrator), and asks for the server's address.
  |
  |2.2 Manual installation
  |In the output folder, you will find the following files:
  |
  |* `k17cv2.dll` - the client, a COM automation add-in whose functions call the server
  |* `k17cv2.bas` - VBA module file created for the add-in
  |* `k17cv2.xlam` - Excel add-in workbook holding that module
  |* `jgxlconfig.dll` - JGraph's server configuration add-in (beside JGraph's launchers), which the installer carries
  |
  |On the client machine, register the client once with `regsvr32 k17cv2.dll` (for the current user
  |when not elevated), then open k17cv2.xlam in Excel or import k17cv2.bas into a workbook's
  |Visual Basic project. Excel installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for
  |Excel register as an administrator.
  |
  |Set the server's address with the Configure button JGraph's server configuration add-in puts on
  |Excel's ribbon, or write it under HKEY_CURRENT_USER\Software\JGraph\Excel Client\k17cv2:
  |ServerURL (http://host:port/<archive>), timeoutSeconds and responseSizeLimit (megabytes).
  |
  |The client needs no JGraph Runtime: it talks to the server over HTTP in MATLAB Production Server's
  |protobuf format, so a MATLAB Production Server instance serves it too.

blank.file.readme.txt	2183 chars, CRLF 40, ends with newline 1
  |k17blank Deployable Archive with Excel Integration
  |
  |Before you can use the functions packaged by the compiler, you need to do the following:
  |
  |* Deploy the server archive (the .ctf compiler.build.productionServerArchive wrote) to a jgraph-mps instance.
  |* Install the Excel client on the client machine.
  |* Configure the client with the address of the server instance.
  |
  |1. Deploy the server archive
  |
  |Copy the archive into the instance's auto_deploy folder; jgraph-mps deploys it as it starts or
  |while it runs (jgraph-mps run <instance>). The instance needs no MATLAB Runtime: it is a JGraph
  |Production Server, which carries its own engine.
  |
  |2. Install the Excel client
  |
  |2.1 Using the installer
  |compiler.package.excelClientForProductionServer writes an installer that copies the client's
  |files, registers k17blank.dll and JGraph's server configuration add-in for the
  |current user (for every user when run as an administrator), and asks for the server's address.
  |
  |2.2 Manual installation
  |In the output folder, you will find the following files:
  |
  |* `k17blank.dll` - the client, a COM automation add-in whose functions call the server
  |* `k17blank.bas` - VBA module file created for the add-in
  |* `k17blank.xlam` - Excel add-in workbook holding that module
  |* `jgxlconfig.dll` - JGraph's server configuration add-in (beside JGraph's launchers), which the installer carries
  |
  |On the client machine, register the client once with `regsvr32 k17blank.dll` (for the current user
  |when not elevated), then open k17blank.xlam in Excel or import k17blank.bas into a workbook's
  |Visual Basic project. Excel installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for
  |Excel register as an administrator.
  |
  |Set the server's address with the Configure button JGraph's server configuration add-in puts on
  |Excel's ribbon, or write it under HKEY_CURRENT_USER\Software\JGraph\Excel Client\k17blank:
  |ServerURL (http://host:port/<archive>), timeoutSeconds and responseSizeLimit (megabytes).
  |
  |The client needs no JGraph Runtime: it talks to the server over HTTP in MATLAB Production Server's
  |protobuf format, so a MATLAB Production Server instance serves it too.

date.file.k17date.bas	1683 chars, CRLF 66, ends with newline 1
  |Dim K17Date As Object 
  |
  |
  |Function k17_none() As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    If K17Date Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17Date = CreateObject("k17date.K17Date")
  |    End If
  |
  |    Call K17Date.k17_none()
  |    k17_none = Empty
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_none = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |
  |Function k17_one(Optional x As Variant) As Variant
  |
  |    Dim y As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    If K17Date Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17Date = CreateObject("k17date.K17Date")
  |    End If
  |
  |    Call K17Date.k17_one(1, y, x)
  |    k17_one = y
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_one = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |
  |Function k17_many(Optional a As Variant, Optional b As Variant, Optional c As Variant) As  _
  |                  Variant
  |
  |    Dim s, d, p As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    If K17Date Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17Date = CreateObject("k17date.K17Date")
  |    End If
  |
  |    ' By default, MATLAB Excel Add-in returns just one output for MATLAB functions 
  |    ' that have more than one output.    
  |
  |    Call K17Date.k17_many(3, s, d, p, a, b, c)
  |    k17_many = s
  |
  |    ' If you want to use all the return values, you can use the following 
  |    ' code as a guideline. To use this form, call the function from Excel cell,
  |    ' highlight the cells which will receive the outputs and hit Ctrl+Shift+Enter.
  |    '
  |    ' Call K17Date.k17_many(3, s, d, p, a, b, c)
  |    ' k17_many = Array(s, d, p)
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_many = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |

numdate.file.k17nd.bas	1649 chars, CRLF 66, ends with newline 1
  |Dim K17Nd As Object 
  |
  |
  |Function k17_none() As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    If K17Nd Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17Nd = CreateObject("k17nd.K17Nd")
  |    End If
  |
  |    Call K17Nd.k17_none()
  |    k17_none = Empty
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_none = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |
  |Function k17_one(Optional x As Variant) As Variant
  |
  |    Dim y As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    If K17Nd Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17Nd = CreateObject("k17nd.K17Nd")
  |    End If
  |
  |    Call K17Nd.k17_one(1, y, x)
  |    k17_one = y
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_one = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |
  |Function k17_many(Optional a As Variant, Optional b As Variant, Optional c As Variant) As  _
  |                  Variant
  |
  |    Dim s, d, p As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    If K17Nd Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17Nd = CreateObject("k17nd.K17Nd")
  |    End If
  |
  |    ' By default, MATLAB Excel Add-in returns just one output for MATLAB functions 
  |    ' that have more than one output.    
  |
  |    Call K17Nd.k17_many(3, s, d, p, a, b, c)
  |    k17_many = s
  |
  |    ' If you want to use all the return values, you can use the following 
  |    ' code as a guideline. To use this form, call the function from Excel cell,
  |    ' highlight the cells which will receive the outputs and hit Ctrl+Shift+Enter.
  |    '
  |    ' Call K17Nd.k17_many(3, s, d, p, a, b, c)
  |    ' k17_many = Array(s, d, p)
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_many = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |
numdate.file.readme.txt	2156 chars, CRLF 40, ends with newline 1
  |k17nd Deployable Archive with Excel Integration
  |
  |Before you can use the functions packaged by the compiler, you need to do the following:
  |
  |* Deploy the server archive (the .ctf compiler.build.productionServerArchive wrote) to a jgraph-mps instance.
  |* Install the Excel client on the client machine.
  |* Configure the client with the address of the server instance.
  |
  |1. Deploy the server archive
  |
  |Copy the archive into the instance's auto_deploy folder; jgraph-mps deploys it as it starts or
  |while it runs (jgraph-mps run <instance>). The instance needs no MATLAB Runtime: it is a JGraph
  |Production Server, which carries its own engine.
  |
  |2. Install the Excel client
  |
  |2.1 Using the installer
  |compiler.package.excelClientForProductionServer writes an installer that copies the client's
  |files, registers k17nd.dll and JGraph's server configuration add-in for the
  |current user (for every user when run as an administrator), and asks for the server's address.
  |
  |2.2 Manual installation
  |In the output folder, you will find the following files:
  |
  |* `k17nd.dll` - the client, a COM automation add-in whose functions call the server
  |* `k17nd.bas` - VBA module file created for the add-in
  |* `k17nd.xlam` - Excel add-in workbook holding that module
  |* `jgxlconfig.dll` - JGraph's server configuration add-in (beside JGraph's launchers), which the installer carries
  |
  |On the client machine, register the client once with `regsvr32 k17nd.dll` (for the current user
  |when not elevated), then open k17nd.xlam in Excel or import k17nd.bas into a workbook's
  |Visual Basic project. Excel installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for
  |Excel register as an administrator.
  |
  |Set the server's address with the Configure button JGraph's server configuration add-in puts on
  |Excel's ribbon, or write it under HKEY_CURRENT_USER\Software\JGraph\Excel Client\k17nd:
  |ServerURL (http://host:port/<archive>), timeoutSeconds and responseSizeLimit (megabytes).
  |
  |The client needs no JGraph Runtime: it talks to the server over HTTP in MATLAB Production Server's
  |protobuf format, so a MATLAB Production Server instance serves it too.

  |    k17_one = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |
  |Function k17_many(Optional a As Variant, Optional b As Variant, Optional c As Variant) As  _
  |                  Variant
  |
  |    Dim s, d, p As Variant
  |
  |    On Error GoTo Handle_Error
  |
  |    If K17All Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17All = CreateObject("k17all.K17All")
  |    End If
  |
  |    ' By default, MATLAB Excel Add-in returns just one output for MATLAB functions 
  |    ' that have more than one output.    
  |
  |    Call K17All.k17_many(3, s, d, p, a, b, c)
  |    k17_many = s
  |
  |    ' If you want to use all the return values, you can use the following 
  |    ' code as a guideline. To use this form, call the function from Excel cell,
  |    ' highlight the cells which will receive the outputs and hit Ctrl+Shift+Enter.
  |    '
  |    ' Call K17All.k17_many(3, s, d, p, a, b, c)
  |    ' k17_many = Array(s, d, p)
  |
  |    Exit Function
  |Handle_Error:
  |    k17_many = "Error in " & Err.Source & ": " & Err.Description
  |End Function
  |
allswitches.file.readme.txt	2165 chars, CRLF 40, ends with newline 1
  |k17all Deployable Archive with Excel Integration
  |
  |Before you can use the functions packaged by the compiler, you need to do the following:
  |
  |* Deploy the server archive (the .ctf compiler.build.productionServerArchive wrote) to a jgraph-mps instance.
  |* Install the Excel client on the client machine.
  |* Configure the client with the address of the server instance.
  |
  |1. Deploy the server archive
  |
  |Copy the archive into the instance's auto_deploy folder; jgraph-mps deploys it as it starts or
  |while it runs (jgraph-mps run <instance>). The instance needs no MATLAB Runtime: it is a JGraph
  |Production Server, which carries its own engine.
  |
  |2. Install the Excel client
  |
  |2.1 Using the installer
  |compiler.package.excelClientForProductionServer writes an installer that copies the client's
  |files, registers k17all.dll and JGraph's server configuration add-in for the
  |current user (for every user when run as an administrator), and asks for the server's address.
  |
  |2.2 Manual installation
  |In the output folder, you will find the following files:
  |
  |* `k17all.dll` - the client, a COM automation add-in whose functions call the server
  |* `k17all.bas` - VBA module file created for the add-in
  |* `k17all.xlam` - Excel add-in workbook holding that module
  |* `jgxlconfig.dll` - JGraph's server configuration add-in (beside JGraph's launchers), which the installer carries
  |
  |On the client machine, register the client once with `regsvr32 k17all.dll` (for the current user
  |when not elevated), then open k17all.xlam in Excel or import k17all.bas into a workbook's
  |Visual Basic project. Excel installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for
  |Excel register as an administrator.
  |
  |Set the server's address with the Configure button JGraph's server configuration add-in puts on
  |Excel's ribbon, or write it under HKEY_CURRENT_USER\Software\JGraph\Excel Client\k17all:
  |ServerURL (http://host:port/<archive>), timeoutSeconds and responseSizeLimit (megabytes).
  |
  |The client needs no JGraph Runtime: it talks to the server over HTTP in MATLAB Production Server's
  |protobuf format, so a MATLAB Production Server instance serves it too.

  |
  |    Dim y As Variant
  |

  |
  |    If K17X Is Nothing Then
  |        Set K17X = CreateObject("k17x.K17X")
  |    End If
  |
  |    Call K17X.k17_one(1, y, x)
  |    k17_one = y

  |End Function

mpsxl_default.file.readme.txt	2147 chars, CRLF 40, ends with newline 1
  |k17x Deployable Archive with Excel Integration
  |
  |Before you can use the functions packaged by the compiler, you need to do the following:
  |
  |* Deploy the server archive (the .ctf compiler.build.productionServerArchive wrote) to a jgraph-mps instance.
  |* Install the Excel client on the client machine.
  |* Configure the client with the address of the server instance.
  |
  |1. Deploy the server archive
  |
  |Copy the archive into the instance's auto_deploy folder; jgraph-mps deploys it as it starts or
  |while it runs (jgraph-mps run <instance>). The instance needs no MATLAB Runtime: it is a JGraph
  |Production Server, which carries its own engine.
  |
  |2. Install the Excel client
  |
  |2.1 Using the installer
  |compiler.package.excelClientForProductionServer writes an installer that copies the client's
  |files, registers k17x.dll and JGraph's server configuration add-in for the
  |current user (for every user when run as an administrator), and asks for the server's address.
  |
  |2.2 Manual installation
  |In the output folder, you will find the following files:
  |
  |* `k17x.dll` - the client, a COM automation add-in whose functions call the server
  |* `k17x.bas` - VBA module file created for the add-in
  |* `k17x.xlam` - Excel add-in workbook holding that module
  |* `jgxlconfig.dll` - JGraph's server configuration add-in (beside JGraph's launchers), which the installer carries
  |
  |On the client machine, register the client once with `regsvr32 k17x.dll` (for the current user
  |when not elevated), then open k17x.xlam in Excel or import k17x.bas into a workbook's
  |Visual Basic project. Excel installed by Click-to-Run sees the machine's registrations alone, so for
  |Excel register as an administrator.
  |
  |Set the server's address with the Configure button JGraph's server configuration add-in puts on
  |Excel's ribbon, or write it under HKEY_CURRENT_USER\Software\JGraph\Excel Client\k17x:
  |ServerURL (http://host:port/<archive>), timeoutSeconds and responseSizeLimit (megabytes).
  |
  |The client needs no JGraph Runtime: it talks to the server over HTTP in MATLAB Production Server's
  |protobuf format, so a MATLAB Production Server instance serves it too.
